THt GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY 


[Part. 1—Sec 1] 


IMPORTS AND EXPORTS (CONTROL) ACT 


Import! and Exports (Conirol) Act, 1947 as amended upio 
30th April, 1979. 

An Act let probibii oi' ttjritr.jl impon^ and cxporiii. 

Whereas it ji expedient ro prohibit, tedriev nr iirbL-twis-c 
control Impotts and exports. 

It is hereby enacted as follows:— 

1. Short title, extent, commencement and duration. —(1) 
This Act may be called the Imports and Exports (Control) 


(2) It extends to Ihe whole of India. 

It shall come inio force on the 2.‘(th day of March, 1947. 

2. Definitions ,—In this Act. unless the context otherwise 
requires.— 

(a) "Adjudicating Authority" means the authorifv speci¬ 
fied in, or under, section 4K; 

(b) “Appellate Authority” means the authoiity referred 
to in section 4M; 

(c) “Chief ControlloP’ means the Chief Controller of 
Imports and Exports; 

(d) "Control Order" means a control order made or 
deemed to have been made under this Act; 

(e) "Customs Station" has the meaning assigned to It 
in the Custonas Act, 1962; 

(f) "Deputy Chief Controller" means a Deputy Chief 
Controller of Imports & Exports; 

fs) “Import” and "fcport” means, respectively, bring¬ 
ing Into, and taking out of India by sea, land or 
air; 

(h) "Letter of Authority" means a letter authorising 
the licensee to permit another person, named in the 
said letter, to Import goods against the licence 
granted to the licensee; 

(i) "licence” meani a licence manted, includes a 
customs clearance permit issued under any control 
order; 

(J) "Prescribed” means prescribed by lailcs made under 
thl® Act; 

(k) "Recognised Agency" means an agency to which the 
functions of dlstributioa of imported goods have 
been assigned by the Chief Controller, 

3. Powers to prohibit or teslrlct imports and export.t.~-(l) 
The Central Government may, by order published In the 
OflBcial Gazette, make provisions for prohibiting, restrlctin.e 
or otherwise controlling in all cases or In speclfl^ classes of 
cases and subject to such exceptions, if any, as may be made 
by or under the order:— 

(a) the Import, export carriage costwlse or shipment ns 
ships stores of goods of any specified description; 

(b) the bringing Into any port or place in India of goods 
of any specified description intended to be taken 
out of India without being removed from the ship 
or conveyance in which they are being carried. 

(2_) All goods to which any order under sub-section fl) 
applies shall be deemed to he goods of which the import or 


export has been prohibited under section 11 of the Custonas 
Act. 1962 ( 52 of 1962), all the provisions of that Act shall 
have effect accordingly, 

(3) Notwithstanding anything contained In the ulcaesaU 
Act, the Central Government may, by order published in th« 
Oflicial Gazette, prohibit, restrict or impose conditions on the 
clearance, whether for homo consumptlrm or for shlpu^nt 
abro.id of any goods or class of goods importetl into India. 

4. Continuation of existing orders .—^All orders made under 
rule S4 of the Defence of India Rules, or that rule as 
continued in force by the Emergency Provisions (ContlnuanceT 
Ordinance, 1946 (Continuance of Existing Orders, XX 
of 1946). and in force immediately before the commencement 
of this Act shall so far as they are not inconsistent with the 
provisions of this Act. continue in force and be deemed 
to have been made under this Act. 

4A. Fee for applications for and issue or renewal of 
licences .—The Central Government may, by order levy, sub¬ 
ject to such exceptions. If any, in respect of any person or 
class of persons as may be specified in the order, any fee in 
respect of any application or m respect of any licence granted 
or renewed under any order made or deemed to have been 
made under this Act. 

4B. Power to enter and Inspect .—^Aay person authorised in 
writing In this behalf by the Chief Controller or any officer 
serving under him, not being an officer below the rank of 
a Deputy Chief Controller (hereinafter in this Act called the 
“authori«Ml person”), may enter, at any reasonable time any 
premises in which— 

(j) any imported goods or materials which are liable 
to confiscation under this Act, or 

(ii) any books of account or other documents or things 
which, in his opinion, will be useful for or relevanl 
to, any proceeding under this Act. 

are suspected to have been kept or concealed and Inspect 
such imported goods, materials, books of account, other 
documents or thinp and may take such notes or extracts 
from such books of account or other documents as he may 
think fit. 

4C. Power to search .—If the authorised person has any 
reason to believe that— 

(i) any imported goods or materials liable to confisca¬ 
tion under this Act, or 

(ii) anj^ books of account or other documents or things 
which, In hi* opinion, will be useful for, or relevant 
to any proceeding under this Act 

are secreted In any place, he may enter into and search such 
place or premises for such imported goods, materials, books 
of account, other documents or things, 

4D. Power to seize imported goods or materials, —(1) if 
the authorised person has any leason to believe that any 
imported goods or materials arc liable to confiscation under 
tills Act, he may seize such goods or material^ together with 
the package, covering or receptacle, if any, in which such 
goods or materials are found to have been mixed with any 
other goods or materials, he may seize such yoods or mate¬ 
rials logethw with the goods or materials with which they 
are so mixed 

Provided that where it is not practicable to seize any such 
goods or materials, the authorised pcison may serve on the 
owner of the goods or materials an order that hp shall not 
remove, part w'ith or otherwise deal with, the goods or mate¬ 
rials except with the previous permission of .sncli authorised 
person.

4G. Confiscation —Any imported goods or materials in 
respect Of which— 

(a) any condition of the licence or letter of authority,

such goods or materials are so mixed with any other goods 
or materials that they cannot be leadily separated such other 
goods or materials shall also be liable lo confiscation : 

Provided that where it is established to the satisfaction of

they belong to a person other than the person who has,_ by

and such act or omission was v/ithout the knowledge or con- 
niv.ance of the person to whom they belong such goods or 
materials shall not he ordered to be confiscated, but such 
other action is authorised by tbi.^ Act may be taken against 
the person who has by such act of omission, rendered such 
goods or materials liable to confiscation. 

4H, Confiscation of conveyance —Any conveyance or

arc liable to confitication under this Act, shall he liable to 
confiscation unless the owner of the conveyance or animal 
proves that it was, is being, or Is about to be so used without 
the knowledge or connivance of the owner himself, bis agent, 
if any, and the person in-charpe of the conveyance or animal 
and that each of them had taken all reasonable precautions 
against such use : 

Provided that in the case of n conveyance or animal used 
for the transport of goods of passengers for hire, the owner of 
the conveyance or animal shall be given an option to pay. In 
lieu of confiscation of the conveyance or animal, a fine not 
exceeding the value of the imported goods or materials which 
have been, are being or attempted to be, transported by such 
conveyance.

IMPORTS (CONTROL) ORDER, 1955 


Government of India 

MINISTRY OE COMMERCE 

Imports (Control) Order, 1955 (No. 17/55) dated the 
7th December, 1955 (as amended upto 30th March, 1990). 

No. 17/55.—In exercise of the powers conferred by sec- 
tions 3 and 4A of the imports and Ex,port8 (Control) Act, 
1947 (18 of 1947) as in force in India and as applied to 
the State of Pondicherry, the Central Government hereby 
makes the loilowinB Order, namely 

ORDER 

1. Short title and Commencement. —(1) This ordw may 
be called the Imports (Control) Order, 1955. 

(2)—It shall come into force at once. 

2. Definition. —In this Order, unless the context otherwise 
requires;— 

(a) ‘the Act’ means the Imports and Exports (Control) 
Act, 1947 (18 of 1947); 

(aa) application for ‘licence’ includes any application 
made under the Import Trade Control rcnulalion; 

(ana) licence includes a Customs Clearance Permit issued 
under this Order; 

(b) ‘licensee’ means a person to whom a licence or a 
Customs Clearance Permit is granted under this 
Order; 

(c) ‘Licensing Authority’ means an authority competent 
to grant a licence or Customs Clearance Permit 
under this Order; 

(d) "An Authorised Oflacer" means an officer not below 
the rank of Deputy Chief Controller of Imports 
and Hxpoits authorised by the Chief Controller of 
imports & Exports. 

(e) ‘Schedule’ means a schedule to this Order; 

(f) ‘Value’ has the same meaning as in sub-section (1) 
of section 14 of the Customs Act 1962 (52 of 
1962). 

(g) Words and expressions used in this order and not 
defined but defined in the Act shall have the mean¬ 
ings respectively assigned to them in the Act. 

3. Restriction of Import of certain goods,—(1) Save as 
otherwise provided in this Order, no person shall import any 
goods of the description specified in Schedule 1. except under 
and in accordance, with a licence or a customs clearance 
permit granted by the Central Government or by any 
Officer specified in Schedule U. 

(2) If in any case, it is found that the goods imported 
under licence do not conform to the description given in the 
licence or were shipped, prior to the date of issue of the 
licence under which they are claimed to have been inipo'lj.: 
then, without prejudice to any action that may be taken 
against the licensee under the Customs Act, 1962 (52 of 
1962), in respect of the said importation the licence may bo 
treated as having been utilised for importing the said goods. 

(3) If, in any case, it is found that the goods imported 
under a licence do not conform in every respect — 

(i) to the description or value of the goods as con¬ 
tained in the licence: or 

(ii) to the other conditions relating to such goods, as 
contained in, or applicable to the licence, 
the import of such goods shall be deemed to be prohibited 


3A. In cases where the importer is unable to produce the 
licence which has already been granted to him at the time 
of arrival of goods, the proper officer of customs may at ins 
discretion allow ffie importer to secure the clearance, of goods 
on execution of a bond or letter of guarantee to the effect 
that he holds a valid licence in respect of the imported 
goods and shall produce the same within a period 
to be specified by the proper officer of customs, fading 
which ho shall pay to proper officer of customs such amount 
as may be stipulated in the bond or letter of guarantee, with¬ 
out prejudice to any action that may be taken against him 
under Customs Act. 1962 (52 of 1962) for unauthorised 
importation of the goods concerned. 

4. Fees on Application of Licence. — (1) Every applicatioa 
for a licence shall bo made to the appropriate liccnsiDg 
authority. 

(2) A fee as indicated in Schedule in shall be paid in 
respect of every applicatioa in the manner provided in the 
said Schedule : 

Provided that no fee shall be payable in respect of anj 
application when made by :— 

(a) the Central Government, a State Government oi 
any department or office of the Central Govern¬ 
ment or State Government; 

(b) any local authority for the import of goods re¬ 
quired for its own consumption: 

(c) any educational, charitable or missionary institution 
for the import of goods required for its own con¬ 
sumption; 

♦The fee once received will not be refunded under any 
ciicumstances except:— 

(i) where the fee has been deposited in excess of the 
prescribed scale: 

(ii) where the fee has been deposited but no applicaliop 
has been made. 

(iii) where the fee has been deposited in error but the 
applicant is exempt from payment of licence fee. 

Note— Fees paid in respect of Appeals made to the Chief 
Controller of imports and Exports and its Regional Licens¬ 
ing Authoiitics shall not be refunded under any circum¬ 
stances. 

5. Conditions of Licences. —(1) The licensing authority 
issuing a licence under this Order may issue the same, subjeci 
to one or more of the conditions stated below 

(i) that the goods covered by the licence shall not be 
disposed of except in the manner pvesciibed by the 
licensing authority or otherwise, dealt with without 
the written permission of the licensing authoritv oj 
any person duly authorised by it; 

(ii) that the goods covered by the licence on importation 
shall not bo sold or distributed at a price exceeding 
that which may be specified in any direction attach¬ 
ed to the licence; 

(iii) that the applicant for a licence shall execute a bond 
for comp tying with the trims subject to which a 
licence may be granted. 

(2) licence granted under this Order shall nliO be sub¬ 
ject to the conditions contained in Schedule V. 

(3) Tt shall be deemed to be a condition of cvi':v sin h 
licence that : — 

(i) no per'^on shall transfci and no person shall acoi.lie 
by transfer ,my licence issneil by the licentiinr 
authority except under and in accordance- with th.,- 
written p'-cmission of the aiithorify v.h'rh gnii',,.

8. Power to debar from importing goods or from receiving 
licences or ai'ioiment of imported goods. —(1) The Central 
Government or the Chief Controller of Imports & Exports

to be not in conformity with any provision of this 
Order; or

which has been tampered with; or 

%

or otherwise; or 

fe) if his agent or employee has been a party to any

(f) if he fails to comply with or contravenes or attempts 
to contravene or abets the contravention of any

or an application for a licence; or

or

concerned; or 

(i) if he fails to comply with the distribution control in 
respect of imported goods where such control is 
applicable.

8, 8A or 8B;

the Customs Act, 1962, or any penalty imposed on

period of three months; and 

(g) if applicant fails to pay any penalty finally imposed 
on him under the Act.

of authority,

BAaCAGB RHl.BS-1978 


(Promulgated by JOl-Cus, dated 16-3-78 as amended by 
Notification 213-Cus. dated 13-11-79, 247-Cu8. dated 26-12-80, 
57/83-Cui. dated 1-3-83, 166/83-Cu». dated 8-6-83, 26/83- 
Cus. dated 4-2-85, 20/86-Cu8, dated 20-1-86, 141/86-Cus. 
dated 13-2-86. 467/86-Cus. dated 23-11-86, 245/87-Cus. dated 
23-6-87, l/88-Cus.-(N,T.) dated 8-1-88 and 47/89-N.T.)- 
Customs dated 14-8-89), 

G.S.R. No. 290(E)—In exercise of powers conferred by 
Bub-section (2) of Section 79 of the Customs Act, 1962 (52 
of 1962), and in supersession of the Baggage Rules, 1970 and 
the Ceylon Baggage Rules, 1930, the Central Government 
hereby makes the following rules, for importing free of duty, 
baggage of passengers (other than tourists) who arrive from 
any country other than Nepal or Bhutan namely ;— 

1, Short Title, Commencement : 

(1) These rules may be called (he Baggage Rules, 1978. 

(2) They shall come into force at once. 

2. Used Personal Effects : The used articles of personal 
wear (excluding jewellery but including not more than one 
wrist watch of vlaue not exceeding five hundred rupeci) 
and articles in personal use of passengers for satisfying daily 
nreosaities of life may be passed free of duty. 

2, General Free Allowance : In addition to the articles 
•pecified in rule 2 and subject to the provisions of rule 9, 
a passenger of and above the age of twelve years, may .also 
be allowed to import free of duty articles :— 


upto a value of thirty rupees in the case of such 
adult passenger and seven rupees fifty palse In the 
ease of such passenger below eighteen years of 
age may only be allowed to be ijnported free of 
duty for each day's stay outside India; 

(iii) El the case of an air passenger who travels on a 
free or concessional ticket and returns to India 
after staying outside India in a counEy other than 
Sri Lanka or Maldives or Nepal or Bhutan for less 
than ten days, articles upto a value of one hundred 
and twenty five rupees in the case of such adult 
pa.ssenger and thirty-rupees ip the case of such 
assenger below eighteen years of age, may only 
e allowed to the imported free of duty for each 
day’s stay outside India. 

Explanation 

For the purposes of this proviso, "concessional ticket" 
means a tllcket issued after allowing a concession of seventy 
five per Cent or more. 

Provided further that subject to the conditions and limita¬ 
tions laid down in this rule, a passenger shall be allowed 
to import free of duty a Colour Television set or Video 
Cassette Recorders or Video Cassette Player or Video 
Camera only iiplo five hundred rupees of Its value; 

Provided also that nothing contained In this rule shall 
apply to any unaccompanied Baggage. 

4. Additional Allowance for used Household Articles 


(a) upto a value of three hundred rupees, in the case 
of a passenger arriving from Sri Lanka, and 

(aa) In the ease of passengers arriving from Maldive.s— 

(i) Upto a value of Rs. 750/. in case the period 
of stay outside India does not exceed three 
days. 

(ii) Upto a value of Rs. 1250/- in case the period 
of such Btay exceeds three days, and 

(b) upto a value of one thousand two hundred and 
fifty rupees, In the case of a passenger arriving 
from a country other than Sri Lanka or Maldives 
or Nepal or Bhutan if the proper officer is satisfied 
that such articles are for the use of the passenger or 
his family or for making gifts or souvenirs; 

Provided that— 

ri) a passenger below twelve years of age may be 
allowed to import free of duty articles— 

(a) upto a value of seventy five rupees, in the 
ca.se of a passenger arriving from Sri Lanka, 

(aa) In the case of pa.ssengers arrivinc fiom Mal¬ 
dives-— 

(i) upto a value of Rs. 200/- in case the 
period of stay outside India does not ex¬ 
ceed three days; 

(ii) upto a value of Rs, 300/- in case the 
period of such stav exceeds three days, 
and 

(b) upto a value of three hundred rupees, in the 
case of a passenger aixivlng from a country 
other than Sri Lanka or Maldives or Nepal or 
Bhutan. 

(ii) in the case of an air passenger who travels on a 
free or concessional ticket and returns to India 
after stayin outside India or in Sri Lanka or 
Maldives or both, for less than ten days, article* 


In the case of a passenger who was engaged In his pro¬ 
fession abroad for over three months, articles actpglly 
used for running his household, namely linen, utensfle, table¬ 
ware, kitchen appliances and iron, upto an aggregate 
value of one thousand rupees may be imported .free »f 
duly. 


4A. Additional Allowance for used Persdnal Effects and 
Household articles In respect of certain categories ef 
passengers 

(1) In the case of a passenger holding a valid passport 
issued^ under the Passports Act, 1967 (13 of 1967) ancl 
returning to India after a period of not less than one year 
of stay abroad, his personal effects and hosehold articles 
upto an aggregate value of twenty thousands mpees, which 
have been in his or his family's possession and use abroad 
for a minimum period of six months may be imported free 
of duty Subject to the following conditions, namely:— 

f() Such passenger ha* been working abroad for a 
minimum period of one year and is returning to 
India on termination of such work; 

fii) Such passenger affirm,s by a declaration that the 
goods in question have been in his or In his 
family's possession and use abroad for a minimum 
period or six months and the examination of such 
goods and the attendent circumstances do not 
indicate to (he contrary; “provided that such 
passenger shall be allowed to avaii himself of the 
provisional of thin rnlc only once in every threij 
yenrs 


(2) Nothing contained in this rule shall apply to 
following articles, namely;—* 

(a) motorcycles, scooters or mopeds; 

(b) aircondltioneri; 

(c) Refrigerators and deep freezers; 

(d) fire arms; 

(dd) catridges of fire arms exceeding ,^0; 

(e) cigarette* exceeding 200 or cigars exceeding 50 
tobacco exceeding 2.^0 grams; and 


the 


or 



[hitI—^ i] 


»TR^f spr TTsitr^r • 


203 


(f) alcoholic liquor in excess of 0,95 litrei. 

(g) video cameras or the combination of any such 
video camera with one or more of the followinR 
goods, namely;— 

(,i) television receivers; 

(ii) sound recording or reproduemg apparatus, 

(iii) video reproducing apparatus; 

(hj compute: s aud computer peripherals (.other itian 
calculating machines). 

(i) word processing machines. 

4B. Manner of Computing the Period of Stay Abroad jor 
the Purposes of Rule 4A in case a passenger has made 
short visits to India during his stay Abroad 

For the purpose of Rule 4-A of these rules, short visits, if 
any, made by the passenger concerned to India during the 
entire period of his stay abroad relevant for the pur¬ 
poses of that rule shall be Ignored if tlte total duration of 
stay In India on these short tnsits does not exceed 45 days- 
subject to the condition that total period of stay abroad, 
excluding the period spent in India on such short 
visits, is not less than 365 days." 

5. Allowance for Professional Equipment 

A passengei^ who was engaged in his profession abroad 
for over three months may be allowed to import free of 
duty such portable equipments, instruments, apparatus and 
appliances as are ordinarily required in such profession, 
uplo a value of five thousand rupees. 

Provided that items of co mm on use like camera, type¬ 
writer, cassette recorder and dictaphone shall not be allowed 
to bo imported free of duty under this rule. 

6. Jewellery 

Jewellery in the actual use of a passenger who has been 
residing abroad for over one year may be allowed to bo 
imported free of duty to the aggregate value of orte thousand 
and five hundred rupees in the case of male passenger and 
three thousand rupees in the case of a female passenger. 

7. Unaccompanied Baggage 

(1) Subject to the conclilious and hmitations laid down 
in these rules, the proper officer may allow import free of 
duty bona fide unaccompanied baggage arilving in India 
after the arrival of the passenger, if it was in bis possession 
abroad and was shipped by sea within the one month or 
despatched by air wilbln a fortnitht of the passenger's arri¬ 
val in India; 

Provided that if the Assistant Collector of Customs or 
the Collector of Customs, as the case may be, is satisfied 
that the passenger could not ship or despatch his bona fide 
unaccompaffied baggage within the period icforcsald, inspite 
of his having taken aU reasonable steps for the purpose, the 
Assistant Collector of Cus'oms may extend the time limit 
of one month to three months or of a fortnight to two 
months as the case may be, and the Collector of Customs 
may extend the time limit upto any further time. 

(2) Bona fide baggage of a passenger landed at any cus¬ 
toms Station within two months before his arrival in India 
may be allowed to be imported free of duty by the proper 
officer subject to the conditions and limitations laid down 
in these rules; 

Provided that if the AsiistaDt Collector of Customs or 
the Collector of Customs, as the case may be, Is satisfied 
for reasons to be recorded in writing, that the passenger 
was prevented from arriving in India within the aforesaid 
period of two months due to circumstances beyond his con¬ 
trol, such as, sudden illness of the passenger or a member 
of his family, or natural calamities :)r dislutbed conditions, 
or disiuption of tin- tr:uisport o: travel airangements in the 
countiy or count: les concerned or any other reasons, which 
necessitated a change in the travel schedule of the passenger. 


the time limit of two months may be extended uplo ■ 
period of — 

(i) four months by the Assistant Collector of Cus¬ 

toms; and 

(ii) one year by the Collector of Customs. 

8. Application of these Rules to Members of the Crew 

Rules 2, 3, 5, 6 and 9 shall apply In respect of officer* 
and members of the crew engaged in a foreign going vessel 
for importation of their baggage at the time of final pay 
off on termination of their engagement. 

Provided that the concessions under rule 5 shall not bo 
allowed except at the time of final pay off on termination of 
first engagement in foreign going vessel. 

9. Articles not Exempted from duty 

Notwithstanding the provisions of rule 3 and 4, the fol¬ 
lowing articles shall not be imported free of duty under 
these rules, namely :— 

(i) motorcycles, scooters or mopcdi; 

(ii) fire-arms; 

U (a) cartridge of fire arms exceeding 50; 

(lii) cigarettes exceeding 200 or cigar* exceeding 50 or 
tobacco exceeding 250 gms; and 

(iv) alcoholic liquor in excess of 0.95 litres. 

10. Allowance not to be availed of both under these rules 
and the Transfer of Residence Rules at the same time 

Notwithstanding anything contained in these rules and the 
Transfer of Residence Rules, 1978, made under section 79 
of the Customs Act, 1962 (52 of 1962); a passenger shall

Short Title 

1. These rule* may be called the Transfer of Residence 
Rules, 1978. 

Personal and Household Ejects Exempted from duty Subject 
to Certain Conditions. 

2. Subject to the provisions of the Baggage (Condi¬ 
tion of Exemption) Rules, 1975 and Rule 3 of these rules 
the personal and household effects of a person on bona-fldo 
transfer of residence to India, shall be exempted from duty 
subject to the following conditions, namely :— 

(a) Such person has been residing abroad for a mini¬ 
mum period of two years immediately preceding 
the transfer of residence and is transferring hli 
residence to India for a minimum stay of one year. 

(b) Such person afiiitns by a declaration that the goods 
have been in his or his family’s possession and use 
abroad for a minimum period of one year and the 
examination of the goods and the attendant cir¬ 
cumstances do not Indicate to the contrary. 

(c) The goods not accompanying the passenger wera 
shipped or despatched or arrived within the time 
limits specified in the Baggage Rules, 1978. 

Explanation : For the purpose of this Rule, the 
expression '‘personal and household effects” shall 
not Include so much of gold jewellery that is to 
say jewellery of which the value is mainly on ac¬ 
count of gold content, as in excess of the value 
of one thousand five hundred rupees in the case

uol^ however, apply to the jewellery in reject of 
which the Assistant Collector of Customs is satis¬

had been token out of India by the passenger or 
by a member of his or her family.

allowed to take the imported goods outside

pose of carrying out sWp repairs. The items 
shall be brought back to the Customs bonded 
premises after carrying out the repairs. In

return shall be submitted to the Collector 
of Customs of the area and Director General 
of Shipping, Bombay. The importer shall 
maintain a proper account which will be 
open to inspection by the Chief Controller 
of Imports and Exports. 

(3) The imported goods shall be used in repairs 
of ocean-going-vessels, whether Indian or 
foreign, in accordance with the provisions of 
.Ministry of Finance, Deportment of Reve¬ 
nue, Notifications No. 21 1|83 -CUSTOMS 
and 212183-CUSTOMS both dated fh* 
23rd July, 1983.

amount of foreign exchange earned in ship 
repairs, duly certified by the Bant, to thp 
licensing authority concerned, and the 
Director General of Shipping, Bombay. This 
return shall be sent through the Customs 
Officer attached to the Unit concerned. 

(5) The importer shall comply with all the con¬ 
ditions laid down in the Re^tration Certi¬ 
ficate, issued to him by Director General 
of Shipping.

cation to any goods, of any other prohibi¬

and Fiji, any goods by Government Departments

namely:—

of cost, in pursuance of an agreement bet- 
wen the Government of India and the 
foreign Government concerned.

the relevant agreement entered into between 
the Government of India and the foreign 
Government concerned and a certificate to 
this effect issued by the administrative; 
Ministry or the Project Authority concern¬ 
ed shall be produced to the Custotns autho¬ 
rities at the time of clearance. 

fiii) Such goods are shipped on through consign¬ 
ment to India on or before the 31st March, 
1993, without any grace period whatso¬ 
ever. 

(iv) The goods so imported have not been pro¬ 
duced or manufactured in the Union of 
South Africa and Fiji. 


2. Under this Licence, import will also be allowed 
of personal effects of foreign expert coming to India 
under Government to Government agreement for the 
execution of a project in India on production of a 
certificate of the Administrative Ministry or the Pro¬ 
ject Authority concerned, at the time of clearance, to 
the effect that imported goods (to be mentioned in 
the certificate) are in accordance with the agreement 
entered into by the Government of India with the 
foreign Government concerned. The import shall 
also be subject to the condition that the goods (other 
than consumables) are io be re-experted as soon as 
the foreign expert concerned leaves India after com¬ 
pletion of his assignment.

tion to any goods, of any other prohibition or regula¬

emption or relaxation at any time from an obligation 
or compliance with any requirement to which the 
Actual Users (Industnal) or the importer may be

porters should comply with the provisions of all other 
laws applicable to them.

S.O. (E).—^In exercise of the powers conferred 
by section 3 of the Imports and Sports (Control) 
Act, 1947 (18 of 1947), the Central Government 
hereby gives general permission, with effect from the 
1st April, 1990 to the 31st March, 1993, to import 
into India from any country exce|:t the Union of 
South Africa and Fiji goods of the description speci¬ 
fied in the Schedule annexed to this Order, as amend¬ 
ed from time to time by issue of a Public Notice in 
the Official Gazette, subject to the following condi¬ 
tions, namely:— 

(1) Except in the case of ‘‘Teaching Aids” 
covered by Serial No, 1 aH other items 
covered by the Schedule annexed to this 
Order, may be imported by any person, for 
slock and sale. 

(2) In the case of “Teaching Aids”, import will 
be allowed only by recognised educational, 
scientific, technical and research institutions, 
libraries of such institutions, Central or 
State Government Departments, industrial 
units engaged in Research and Development 
work, registered medical institutions, hos¬ 
pitals, consultants, recognised Chambers of 
Commerce, productivity councils, manage¬ 
ment associations and professional bodies, 
for tl'.elr own use. Importers of films (whe¬ 
ther scientific or video) as teaching aids, 
shall produce to the Custom authorities a 
certificate from the Central Board of Film 
Certification that the films imported are 
‘predominantly educational’, 

(3) In the case of pulses, all eligible importers 
shall be required to register their contracts 
with the National Agricultural Cooperative 
Marketing Federation of India (NAFED). 
Import shall be made only after the connect¬ 
ed contracts have been stamped by NAFED 
as an evidence of such registration. For 
this purpose, two copies of the contract 
should be lodged with the NAFFD ; they 
will return one copy to the importer, duly 
stamped on each page, for production to 
the Customs authorities at the time of 
clearance of goods. 


(4) In the case of drug items, the requirements 
regarding possession of a valid licence under 
the Drugs and Cosmetics Act, 1940, 
(23 of 1940) wherever necessary, should be 
complied with. 

(5) In the case of dates, homeopathic medicines, 
anticancer drugs, life saving drugs and crude 
drugs, import will also be allowed in tradi¬ 
tional small packing. 

(6) (1) Importers of Life Saving Equipment 

appearing in List 2 of Appendix 6 of 
Import and Export Policy, 1990—93 
(Volume I) shall be required to produce 
the valid Certificate issued by the 
Director General of Technical Develop¬ 
ment or by any other agency designated 
for the purpose by the Chief Control¬ 
ler of Imports and Exports, New Delhi, 
at the time of customs clearance. 

(2) Importers of Life Saving Equipment 
shall be eligible to import spares of such 
equipment either olongwith the machines 
or separately. 

(7) Goods imported under Open General 
Licence are not permissible for export with¬ 
out the written permission of the Chief 
Controller of Imports and Exports, New 
Delhi. 

(8) The goods so imported have not been pro¬ 
duced or manufactured in the Union of 
South Africa and Fiji.

equipment including systems and devices

nate Sources of Energy, the importer shall
